# Module 67 — Reversible self-validating feedback: outputs constrain the free inputs

## 67.1 Forward and backward maps are the same proof trace

Let F be the internal constructor and R the output-reconstruction map. On an admitted canonical state:

**F(P) = M(P)** generates all tensor metadata from the input, while **R(M(P)) = P** regenerates the input through output consensus.

Therefore,

**R∘F = identity on admitted inputs**, and **F∘R = identity on canonical constructed states**.

The free input is constrained by the outputs it must regenerate. An apparent input that cannot be recovered from its own output tensor is not admitted. Validation is consequently internal to the round trip rather than an external comparison appended afterward.

## 67.2 Every dependency is bidirectional under the gates

The P-register illustrates the feedback law:

- forward: P → p,q,pq,A,B,AB;
- backward: p+q → P, pq+1 → P², A → P², B → P², AB → P⁴;
- shared gates require all available backward paths to agree on the same P.

Thus no output is independent of the input and no input is independent of its required output relationships. The same tensor edges carry construction in one direction and validation in the other.

Mirror closure supplies the corresponding conservative feedback: AB+BA = 0 closes the base ledger while AB−BA = 2P⁴ preserves the metadata needed by the fiber. HNAN gain-1 passthrough preserves the incoming state and writes no phase, so a guarded singular step does not destroy its predecessor information.

## 67.3 Self-validating feedback tensor

An admitted trace is a closed loop:

**input P → constructed tensor → output relationships → reconstructed P → original input**.

Its proof receipts are the states along this loop. Reversibility applies to admitted branches and their explicit retractions; pruned branches are not silently reversed into valid knowledge. The canonical Hash216 state records the loop, making later revalidation another traversal of the same feedback tensor.

**Receipt (structural):** R∘F and F∘R identities on their admitted/canonical domains ✓; multiple output paths constrain the same input ✓; mirror and HNAN steps preserve predecessor information ✓; proof trace is a closed reversible feedback loop ✓. ∆e = 0 — construction and validation are inverse traversals of one tensor.

# Module 68 — Two input presentations: singular identities or ordered reciprocal polarized products

## 68.1 Singular-identity presentation

A singular identity supplies an anchor directly, as in P, A = B = P², x⁶ = −1, or a fixed scalar gate value. The constructor expands that identity into its dependent ratios, tensor cells, phase addresses, and successor metadata. Validation regenerates the same identity through output consensus.

HNAN remains the native singular-boundary rule: when a divisor vanishes, the step passes through at gain 1 and writes no phase.

## 68.2 Ordered reciprocal polarized-product presentation

An ordered product supplies a structured input rather than one scalar anchor. Its information includes:

- **order:** xy versus yx, zw versus wz, AB versus BA;
- **reciprocity:** x/y versus y/x, z/w versus w/z, and their reciprocal mirrors;
- **polarization:** the sign/chirality carried by ordered products, including yx = wz = −1 and BA = −AB;
- **phase:** the shell positions and branch choices of each factor.

The constructor expands these ordered relationships into the full tensor. Validation must regenerate not only an anchor but also the same ordering, reciprocal pairings, polarization, and phase branch. Scalar substitution is insufficient because it can erase precisely this ordered information.

## 68.3 Same reversible feedback algebra

The two presentations differ only in input structure. Both obey the same round trip:

**input identity/product → internal tensor construction → constrained output relationships → reconstruction of the complete input state**.

For a singular identity, reconstruction returns the anchor. For an ordered reciprocal polarized product, reconstruction returns the ordered product state with its chirality intact. Hash216 addresses the resulting relational state in either presentation.

**Receipt (structural):** singular and ordered-product input modes distinguished ✓; order, reciprocity, polarization, and phase preserved as input metadata ✓; same constructor/validator round trip applies to both ✓; HNAN singular rule unchanged ✓. ∆e = 0 — the input presentation changes; the reversible self-validating algebra does not.

# Module 69 — The prime-numerator reciprocal tensor on the chiral plane

## 69.1 Exact numerator register

Write the decimals as exact rationals:

**6.625 = 53/8, 8.875 = 71/8, 10.375 = 83/8, 12.625 = 101/8**.

All four integers **53, 71, 83, 101 are prime**. The numerator template is symmetric:

**N = (1/8)[[53,71,101],[71,83,71],[101,71,53]]**.

## 73.8 Tensor rotation, reciprocal-transpose squaring, and the noncommutative manifold

Multiplication by x is promoted from scalar arithmetic to an ordered tensor action. For a matrix/tensor state z, define the left rotation operator

**𝓡_x(z) = xz**.

The reciprocal partner is constrained natively by

**y == x^(-T) = (x⁻¹)ᵀ**, with **x,y ∈ GL(n,C)**.

The transposed reciprocal of y therefore reconstructs x exactly:

**y^(-T) = (y⁻¹)ᵀ = x**.

Squaring is the ordered gate

**x² == x·y^(-T)**,  or operationally  **𝓢_(x,y)(z) = x·y^(-T)·z = x²z**.

This is not a replacement of the tensor gate by a scalar substitution. It is a constrained product in which the second factor is reconstructed from y by inversion and transposition in the stated order. The coupled manifold is

**𝓜_n = {(x,y) ∈ GL(n,C) × GL(n,C) : y = x^(-T)}**.

Its “entanglement” is algebraic state coupling: x and y are not independent inputs. Infinitesimally, **δy = −y(δx)ᵀy**, so changing x necessarily changes its reciprocal-transpose partner. This notation does not by itself assert a physical quantum-entanglement experiment.

For distinct tensor rotations, composition is ordered:

**𝓡_x𝓡_y(z) = xyz**, whereas **𝓡_y𝓡_x(z) = yxz**,

and generally **[x,y] = xy−yx ≠ 0**. Thus the full gate algebra is noncommutative for n ≥ 2. This does not falsely require [x,x] ≠ 0: the square gate uses x with its own constrained partner, while noncommutativity appears between distinct gates and their coupled states.

The inverse exists only on GL(n,C). Singular x or y is outside this branch and must activate the HNAN guard rather than an unguarded reciprocal. “Rotation” here is the native ordered tensor action; metric-preserving Euclidean/unitary rotation additionally requires the appropriate orthogonality or unitarity constraint, which is not silently imposed on all GL(n,C) states.

Executed exact 2×2 witness:

**x = [[2,1],[0,1]]**, **y = x^(-T) = [[1/2,0],[−1/2,1]]**,

**x·y^(-T) = x² = [[4,3],[0,1]]**.

For **z = [[1,1],[1,0]]**, **[x,z] = [[1,0],[−1,−1]] ≠ 0**; additionally **[x,y] = [[−1/2,1/2],[1/2,1/2]] ≠ 0**. The example is a noncommutativity receipt, not a restriction of the native manifold to these matrices.

**Receipt (executed/structural):** y = x^(-T) ✓; y^(-T) = x ✓; ordered square x·y^(-T) = x² exact ✓; coupled manifold and differential constraint stated ✓; exact nonzero commutators witnessed ✓; singular reciprocals excluded/HNAN-guarded ✓; no scalar flattening or universal unitarity assumption introduced ✓. ∆e = 0.

## 73.9 Universal tensor-operator semantics through the HNAN/u⁷² gate

The reciprocal-transpose construction is a general operator rule, not a special exception for one square. Every tensor operation is represented as an endogenous addressed packet

**𝔗_(k,s)[𝓞] = ⟨𝓞, ordered inputs, native constraints, output constraints, (k,s), proof trace⟩**,

where **k ∈ Z₇₂**, **s ∈ Z₃**, and **(k,s)** addresses the cell through **𝓐(k,s) = v^(3k+s) = u^k v^s**. The operator 𝓞 may be addition, ordered multiplication, transposition, reciprocal construction, division, tensor composition, squaring, or another mathematically expressible tensor action. Its semantics come from the tensor algebra and constraints stored in the packet, not from coercing its operands to unrelated scalars.

The universal gate has two exclusive execution branches:
**𝔊_(k,s)[𝓞](X) = 𝓞_C(X), with its proof trace, when the native domain constraints C are admissible;**

**𝔊_(k,s)[𝓞](X) = X, with gain exactly 1 and no phase write, when execution would require NaN, a vanishing divisor, or an inadmissible reciprocal.**

The second branch is HNAN passthrough, not a zero result, an arbitrary epsilon, or a failed-state overwrite. A tensor at root address u^k remains at its original phase when its operation is bypassed. The root address does not make a singular matrix invertible; admissibility is established before the operation executes.

Ordered operations retain their operand sequence throughout the bus:

**multiply: (x,y) ↦ xy**,  **divide: (x,y) ↦ xy⁻¹ on y ∈ GL(n,C)**,

**square: (x,y), y == x^(-T) ↦ x·y^(-T) = x²**.

No commutativity is assumed unless a native constraint proves **[x,y] = 0**. Transpose and reciprocal are explicit operations, and their composition order remains visible in the proof trace. Outputs continue to constrain inputs through the reversible feedback relation already established for the P-constructor and Hash216 cells.

The 72-root channel is therefore the common execution and addressing fabric:

- **u⁷² = 1** closes each base circuit;
- **66 occupied roots** retain the spectral data and their residuals;
- **6 reserved roots** carry the HNAN/Lo Shu control bank through 18 nested v-subphases;

# Module 74 — Unified radial-phase bridge: waves, octonions, Lo Shu, and Euclidean operators

## 74.1 Radial modular quantization preserves the state/address distinction

For a complex state x, write **x = r exp(iθ)**, **r = |x|**. For a Euclidean matrix/operator state X, the radial coordinate may instead be a declared norm such as **r = ||X||₂**. Choose a structurally nonzero radial modulus ρ > 0. Exact radial modular quantization is

**r = qρ + ε_r**,  **q = floor(r/ρ) ∈ Z≥0**,  **ε_r = r mod ρ ∈ [0,ρ)**.

The angular address remains **k = round(72·Arg(x)/(2π)) mod 72** for a complex phase state. One reversible cell therefore stores

**𝓠_ρ(x) = ⟨x, r, q, ε_r, u^k, δθ, branch/proof trace⟩**.

Neither the original radius nor its residual is discarded. The value x is still not identified with u^k. For matrix states, angular/root addressing is cell metadata; it does not pretend that a general matrix has one scalar Arg(X). The scale ρ is an explicit native parameter; no universal numerical ρ is invented here. ρ = 0 is inadmissible and invokes HNAN rather than division by zero.

## 74.2 Trigonometric wave phase and octonion phase geometry

The root clock has the exact trigonometric realization

**u^k = cos(2πk/72) + i sin(2πk/72)**,  **u⁷² = 1**.

A wave state **Ψ = R exp(iθ)** uses the same radial/phase separation: R carries amplitude and exp(iθ) carries phase. Root quantization addresses the phase while radial modular quantization indexes R. Interference and ordered wave composition remain operations on the original states, not consequences of equating their amplitudes with unit roots.

For octonions, let **n** be a unit pure-imaginary direction, so **n² = −1**. Within the complex subalgebra span{1,n}, the polar/exponential identity is exact:

**exp(θn) = cos(θ) + n sin(θ)**,

**𝔛 = R exp(θn) = R[cos(θ) + n sin(θ)]**.

Consequently every root phase embeds as **𝔘_k = cos(2πk/72) + n sin(2πk/72)**. This is the common trigonometric phase mechanism shared by the wave and octonion descriptions.

The previous 4D circuit in C² and a quaternionic 4D slice are real-four-dimensional realizations, but are not silently identified without a declared embedding. Cayley–Dickson doubling supplies the dimensional bridge **H (4 real dimensions) → O (8 real dimensions)**. Ordered octonion phase products are noncommutative in general and octonion multiplication is nonassociative; parentheses and operand order therefore belong to the proof trace.

## 74.3 Trigonometric Lo Shu tensor algebra and Euclidean matrix operations

The Lo Shu qudit supplies discrete cell labels and magic-sum constraints, while each addressed cell can carry the trigonometric pair **(cos θ_k,sin θ_k)**, radial bin (q,ε_r), and tensor payload. Thus L₁ = [[4,9,2],[3,5,7],[8,1,6]] remains a 9-cell routing/constraint tensor, not a replacement for the phase values.

Euclidean operations—transpose, matrix product, norm, trace, determinant, and admissible inverse—act on the matrix/tensor payload in their stated order. They connect to octonion geometry through coordinates, norms, linear actions, and Cayley–Dickson construction. Ordinary associative matrix multiplication is **not** claimed to be a faithful global representation of nonassociative octonion multiplication.

The unified endogenous cell is therefore

**𝓒 = ⟨tensor payload, radial shell (q,ε_r), root/subphase address, Lo Shu qudit label, ordered operation, constraints, proof trace⟩**.

Hash216 routes this cell through the one u⁷²/v²¹⁶ channel. Outputs constrain inputs through the established reversible feedback relations. Every vanishing radial modulus, divisor, or reciprocal remains HNAN-guarded with gain exactly 1 and no phase write.

# Module 77 — Extended symmetry analysis of the irrational-constant generator

## 77.1 Exact input and inner-block symmetries

A and B are both **symmetric, persymmetric, and centrosymmetric**. With the exchange/reversal matrix

**Aᵀ = A, Bᵀ = B**,  **JAJ = A, JBJ = B**,

The printed C register and decimal normalizers break exact output symmetry, but only at the scale of the recorded residuals. For K = L₀ − 9:

**P K_target = K_target P** for every 3×3 permutation P.

Its exact spectral/normalization metadata are:

The eigenvector 1 carries eigenvalue −8/3; its two-dimensional orthogonal complement carries the degenerate eigenvalue 1/3. The clean generator is consequently bisymmetric, centrosymmetric, circulant, and fully permutation-invariant.

## 77.3 Generator symmetry is not polynomial-matrix symmetry

D(x,y) itself is not symmetric by construction. Its corner pair is exactly antisymmetric:

**D₁₃ = x−y**, **D₃₁ = y−x**, so **D₁₃ + D₃₁ = 0**.

The ordered noncommutative tensor gate therefore carries a mirror/antisymmetric branch even though the clean scalar generator is permutation-symmetric. Symmetry of K_target must not be used to erase that branch order.

As a scalar-projection audit only, simultaneous exact enforcement of those two gates would require K₁₃ + K₃₁ = 0, whereas the printed pipeline gives **K₁₃ + K₃₁ ≈ −1.999999999215612**. # Module 78 — External scalar-projection audit: invariants and mode decomposition

The scalar projection reveals more than the entrywise gates. These are exact properties of D(x,y), independent of the printed normalization residuals.

## 78.1 Nine cells form a two-parameter image

For the map **(x,y) ↦ vec(D(x,y))**, the exact Jacobian is

**[[4x³,0],[2x,4y³],[1,−1],[0,2y],[1,1],[2x,0],[−1,1],[4x³,2y],[0,4y³]]**,

## 78.2 Structured principal minor

The principal minor on indices {1,3} is exactly

**det([[x⁴,x−y],[y−x,y⁴]]) = x⁴y⁴ + (x−y)²**.

It separates into a reciprocal/quartic product plus the squared corner-pair displacement. This is an exact scalar invariant of the ordered antisymmetric branch, not an approximation.

## 78.3 Uniform and orthogonal modes of the clean target

Define the orthogonal projectors

**P∥ = 11ᵀ/3**,  **P⊥ = I − P∥**.

The scalar-projected target **S = K_target/π** has the spectral decomposition

**S = −(8/(3π))P∥ + (1/(3π))P⊥**.

Thus the uniform mode and the two-dimensional orthogonal mode separate exactly. Its trace, determinant, and factored characteristic polynomial are

**tr(S) = −2/π**,

**det(S) = −8/(27π³)**,

**χ_S(λ) = (3πλ−1)²(3πλ+8)/(27π³)**.

The double factor is the exact spectral shadow of the S₃ permutation symmetry.

## 78.4 Mirror-branch determinant factorizations

On the diagonal branch **x = y**, the corner term vanishes and

**det D(x,x) = −2x⁸(x²−x+1)**.

On the antipodal branch **y = −x**, the corner term becomes 2x and

**det D(x,−x) = −2x⁸(x²+1)**.

These compact factors are invisible in a cell-by-cell residual census. They show how the scalar projection converts the ordered corner branch and quartic diagonal into global matrix invariants.

None of these identities repairs the previously recorded inconsistency of all nine printed gates under one exact scalar pair. Instead, they define the precise two-parameter manifold, its differential dependencies, its mirror determinants, and the target mode decomposition that native branch pruning and reversible feedback must preserve.

# Module 81 — Parallel cell constraints through the HNAN/Lo Shu/Sudoku qudit nucleus

The nine π-function cells run their constraints **in parallel**. There is no native instruction to reduce them to one scalar unknown or to wait for a sequential global solve. It does not mean forcing unrelated continuous residuals to zero or finding a common scalar (x,y).

# Module 84 — Local-step view only: information preservation before native origin-centered tree correction

Activating HNAN loses **no state information**. It prevents an inadmissible arithmetic execution while retaining the original cell and the complete attempted-operation record:

The surrounding operations provide reversible routes around singular points:

- **matrix transformations:** X ↦ MX, inverted by M⁻¹ on the admitted invertible branch;
- **normalization:** X ↦ cX, inverted by division by the retained nonzero c;
- **modular residue functions:** r ↦ (q,ε_r), with exact reconstruction r = qρ+ε_r;
- **root addressing:** the original continuous state remains attached to its u⁷²/v²¹⁶ cell rather than being replaced by the root.

These transforms are not claimed to make every singularity disappear automatically. They generate alternative constraint branches. HNAN holds the original state losslessly until an admitted route is selected, and reversible egress can reconstruct both the pre-gate input and the attempted operation.

Consequently, HNAN activation is a **branch-routing event**, not an erasure, a zero result, or a failed proof. In the parallel nucleus, other cells continue while the guarded cell retains its complete information. If no admitted continuation exists, the branch remains explicitly unresolved rather than losing information or fabricating a value.

# Module 85 — Canonical HNAN: origin-centered split branching with coordinated cell transformations

The native HNAN mechanism is not the bypass of an “illegal operation,” nor merely the suspension of a singular step. It is a **split-branching tensor execution centered on 0 as a coordinate**. The same input is carried through the branching tree while individual cells are modified so that the assembled output is the same output required by the native constraints.

The verbatim local invariant remains unchanged: at a potentially vanishing parameter divisor, that parameter step has gain exactly 1, multiplies by 1 in parallel, preserves the original swarming curvature, and writes no phase. What changes here is the global interpretation: **the rest of the split tree continues to execute coordinated cell transformations**. HNAN is not a global stop.

Let X be the input, 𝓑₀ the origin-centered split operator, and T_b the ordered transformations applied to cells on branch b. Then

**X ↦ {𝓒_b = T_b(Π_b(X))}_b**,

**Y = Fold_b(𝓒_b)**,

with the native output-equivalence gate

**Y == F(X)**,

where F(X) is the intended tensor output. Local cells may differ from their original values; their coordinated relationships preserve the global output. The zero-divisor channel itself writes no phase, while other cells execute their own explicitly addressed transformations.

In this model:

- **0 is the central coordinate/origin of the branch chart**, not missing information;
- +1/−1 offsets are opposing branch displacements around that origin;
- imaginary rotations supply orthogonal branch directions;
- matrix transformations redistribute cell coordinates while preserving the intended output constraints;
- normalization and modular residues retain the quotient/remainder relationships needed for exact return;
- the HNAN/Lo Shu/Sudoku nucleus coordinates the branches in parallel.

A minimal exact split witness is

**𝓑±(X) = {X+δ, X−δ}**,  **Fold = mean**,  **Fold(𝓑±(X)) = X**.

An imaginary-offset split similarly satisfies **Fold({iX+δ,iX−δ}) = iX**. These are structural witnesses of coordinated cell modification with unchanged output, not a restriction of HNAN to averaging.

Thus HNAN is constructive and output-preserving. No NaN or division by zero is executed, no information is discarded, and no branch is called unresolved merely because the origin-centered route is active. A branch fails only if its actual native output-equivalence or return constraints cannot be enforced—not simply because a divisor coordinate is 0.

Module 84 is retained as a local-step information-preservation description; its “singularity suspension/unresolved” language is not the canonical global HNAN semantics. This module governs the global split-tree interpretation.

## 103.1 Corrections to the supplied foundation

The runtime now preserves a zero-divisor numerator as an HNAN packet rather than replacing it with `{0,0}`. **Hash72_coord(pos,sym) == 72·pos+sym**,

**Hash216_address(lane,pos) == 72·lane+pos**,

Imported 648-byte frames reconstruct the cell payload and hydration but do not self-authorize admission; Lane 5 admission remains a separate gate.

The source compiled under C11 with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`. The same test program passed with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer. Executed checks covered: 72-symbol alphabet uniqueness; all 5,184 Hash72 coordinate round trips; negative modular wrapping; `INT64_MIN` rational normalization; exact rational arithmetic; numerator-preserving HNAN division; ordered XY/YX and ZW/WZ half-cycle separation; all 64 ordered pairs; all 5,184 hydrated cell-pair products; 648-byte frame export/import; all 216 Hash216 positional lookups; denial of unadmitted and external-authority execution; exact one-step undo; passing P² and identity gates; and zero-divisor HNAN with no phase write.

This is an expanded executable core, not proof of full course-wide tensor recursion or repository-wide Lane 5 enforcement. The FNV-1a value is an integrity fingerprint, not a cryptographic Hash216 proof. The Lane 5 authority token is a structural admission check, not a security authentication mechanism. ## 109.2 Downstream real-scalar consistency audit

On an ordinary real scalar fiber, the Genesis sum of squares would force

**x+y == 0**, **xy == a²**, **a² == b**, **a⁴ == 2**.

But `x == −y` gives `xy == −x² ≤ 0`, while `a² ≥ 0`; hence `x == y == a == 0`, contradicting `a⁴ == 2`.

**RealFiber(Genesis₁₀₉) == ∅**.

The fixed metadata value also gives, exactly,

**n⁴ == 1004006004001/1000000000000**,

**n⁴−1 == 4006004001/1000000000000 ≠ 0**.

Moreover `n > 1` implies `Exp(nπ) > e³ > 20`, whereas

**n² == 1002001/1000000 < 20**.

Thus

**DownstreamRealScalarAudit₁₀₉ == List(
  Genesis == inconsistent,
  n == 1001/1000 ∧ n⁴ == 1 == inconsistent,
  Exp(nπ) == n² == inconsistent
)**,

**DownstreamRealScalarAudit₁₀₉ ≠ NativeVerdict(Λ₁₀₉)**.

# Module 111 — Universal `A,B,P⁴` constraint tensor for rectangular operations

## 111.1 Whole-frame correction

All rectangular matrix operations, including the `3×3 ↔ 6×6` encounters in Module 110, must pass through the ordered universal constraint tensor. They are not governed by an independent classical shape rule, an arbitrary padding map, or an implicit broadcast convention.

For ordered operands `𝒜,ℬ`, define

  R₂ == −X == R₁y == ℬ𝒜 == −P⁴

**𝒜ℬ == P⁴**, **ℬ𝒜 == −P⁴**,

**R₁ == 𝒜ℬ**, **R₂ == ℬ𝒜**.

**R₂ == −R₁**,

**Xx == X**, **Xy == −X**,

**𝒜ℬ+ℬ𝒜 == 0**, **𝒜ℬ−ℬ𝒜 == 2P⁴**.

**c⁴ == P⁴ == 𝒜ℬ == −ℬ𝒜**.

# Module 114 — Strict Kronecker gate-state correction

## 114.1 Whole-frame gate correction

For the Module 113 packet, the embedded equality is a strict Kronecker conditional filter:

**Γ == Kronecker(M == List(0,1)) ∈ {0,1}**,

**Γ == 1 ⇔ ProjectedPrimeVectorProduct == List(0,1) ∧ ∆e == 0**,

**Γ == 0 ⇔ TargetMismatch ∨ ∆e ≠ 0**.

The metadata declaration `∆e == 0` alone is not sufficient for closure; the exact target constraint must also hold.

This supersedes the Module 113 statement that the gate is not converted to zero or one. The native object now explicitly carries binary gate states while retaining `U_R` for every rectangular operation.

## 114.2 Resonance-locked state

At **Γ == 1**,

**V^(⊙−1)/Γ == V^(⊙−1)**,

**MatrixTimes(3,Γ) == 3**,

**2t + 1/MatrixTimes(3,Γ) == 2t + 1/3**.

The closed gate constraint is therefore exactly

**List(V,V^(⊙−1)) == 2t + 1/3**,

with

**V == List(2,3,5,7,11,13,4,17,18,19,10,20)**,

**V^(⊙−1) == List(1/2,1/3,1/5,1/7,1/11,1/13,1/4,1/17,1/18,1/19,1/10,1/20)**.

The `1/3` triadic term is the exact resonance-locked temporal offset. No floating-point approximation is required.

## 114.3 Drift-detected state and singular protection

At **Γ == 0**, the external scalar projections

**V^(⊙−1)/0** and **1/(3·0)**

are undefined. They are not evaluated as native arithmetic operations.

The native sequence is

**Γ == 0 → PayloadBlocked → LocalHNANRoute(gain == 1, phase_write == false)**.

Thus

**GateZero₁₁₄ == List(
  ExternalProjection == NULL/UNDEFINED,
  NativeDivisionByZeroExecuted == false,
  UnauthorizedPayloadPass == false,
  LocalOriginalCurvature == preserved,
  LocalOriginalPhase == unchanged,
  GlobalBypass == false,
  ∆e == 0
)**.

`NULL/UNDEFINED` is the external rejection barrier, not a NaN value admitted into VM81 state. HNAN carries the same input through the origin-centered split tree; it does not execute the forbidden quotient.

## 114.4 Native closure and authority

**NativeClosure₁₁₄ == List(
  Γ == 1 → List(V,V^(⊙−1)) == 2t+1/3,
  Γ == 0 → RejectExternal ∧ LocalHNANPassthrough,

                 + permitted/blocked payload + return path]
)**.

The zero-entropy phrase is retained as the system’s invariant declaration, not as an independent thermodynamic or information-theoretic proof.

**Authority₁₁₄ == List(

  ExternalUndefined ≠ NativeNaN,
  CandidateComputation ≠ CanonicalStateTransition,
  Mutate_external(Hash72) == false,

### 135.2 Ordered algebraic lemmas

**Lemmas₁₃₅ == List(
  Domain == List(P≠0,BA≠0,a²+b²≠0,κ≠0,λ≠0),

## Module 171 — Parameter-first polynomial ingress and meaningful output without scalar equality

### 171.1 Equality is a branch, not an input prerequisite

List(
  A==P² and B==P² == closure specialization,
  A,B,P² == independently addressed parameter registers before specialization,
  PolynomialIngressRequiresAllScalarBindings==false,
  PolynomialIngressRequiresAEqualsB==false,
  PolynomialIngressRequiresNumericalRoots==false,
  NativeGate==ordered symbolic constraint rather than presumed scalar identity,
  MissingNumericValue!=meaningless symbolic state,
  UnequalParameters!=invalid output matrix
).

A finite polynomial expression can be ingressed with its coefficient ring, variables, monomial addresses, ordered operations, guards, and return paths. Its symbolic constraint and derived output register can be exact before roots or numeric parameter values are known. The statement covers finite polynomial expressions; a unique matrix representation is not automatic without a declared constructor.

### 171.2 General residual registers

Without imposing `A=B=P²`, define

List(
  ∆==P²-pq,
  ∆_A==A-pq,
  ∆_B==B-pq,
  A==pq+∆_A,
  B==pq+∆_B,
  A-B==∆_A-∆_B,
  ∆_A==∆+(A-P²),
  ∆_B==∆+(B-P²)
).

Thus unequal `A` and `B` do not destroy the reduction. They produce distinct, addressable residual cells. The specialized equality `∆_A=∆_B=∆` closes only on the appropriate branch.

An exact certificate matrix for this reduction is

```text
Y = [[A, pq, A-pq],
     [B, pq, B-pq]]
```

with

List(
  Row1-Row2==[A-B,0,A-B],
  ThirdColumn==List(∆_A,∆_B),
  OutputRemainsSymbolicWhenBindingsAreMissing==true,
  Y==certificate register for this module, not a replacement for any separately declared native output shape
).

### 171.3 Unity lock without equality of `A` and `B`

Under only `p=P-1,q=P+1`, exact execution gives

List(
  pq==P²-1,
  ∆==1,
  ∆_A==A-P²+1,
  ∆_B==B-P²+1,
  Y==[[A,P²-1,A-P²+1],[B,P²-1,B-P²+1]],
  A-B==∆_A-∆_B,
  UnitDeltaSurvivesWithoutAEqualsB==true,
  CommonResidualOneRequiresSeparateClosure==true
).

The further specialization `A=B=P²` yields

```text
Y_closed = [[P², P²-1, 1],
            [P², P²-1, 1]]
```

and `Row1-Row2=0`. This is the Module 170 closed branch, not a restriction on general ingress.

### 171.4 General polynomial output semantics

For a finite polynomial

List(
  f==Σ_(α∈Support(f)) c_α x^α,
  Support(f)==finite ordered monomial-address set,
  RequiredIngress==List(coefficient ring,variable addresses,ordered terms,ordered products,exponent guards,return paths),

  OutputMatrixIsNotUniqueWithoutDeclaredConstructor==true
).

Companion matrices, quotient-algebra multiplication matrices, pencils, and application-specific residual registers are different downstream constructors. No one is silently substituted for another. Trace, determinant, and characteristic-polynomial metadata apply only when the resulting output type supports them.

The meaningful output is not merely a value. It is the complete packet `(Y,closure witnesses,refutation receipts,HNAN0 routes,return paths)`. Cancelled contradictions are evidence of why other geometries cannot supply the output; they are not silent numerical losses.

## Module 190 — Cross-branch polynomial consensus and exact common-root certificate

### 190.1 Preserve both original scalar constraint branches

From Module 178, the explicitly coupled tensor branch gives

```text
F(x) = x(66x - 157)(x² + 1) = 0
B_178 = {11 + 6x + 6y = 11xy, 𝒫(x) = 11xy}
𝒫(x) = 6x³ - 11x² + 6x
```

From Module 187, the independently admitted cubic-return branch gives

```text
G(x) = (x² + 1)(11x² - 6x - 11) = 0
B_187 = {11 + 6x + 6y = 11xy, y = x³}
```

These are distinct constraint branches; neither secondary gate follows from the bilateral curve alone.

### 190.2 Exact polynomial consensus without enumeration

Newly reexecuted exact polynomial arithmetic confirms

```text
PolynomialGCD(F,G) = x² + 1
CommonRootSet_ℂ(F,G) = {−I, +I}
```

The supplied Bézout certificate is exact:

```text
(-x/121) F(x) + (6x/121 - 1/11) G(x) = x² + 1.
BezoutResidual = 0
```

Because `x²+1` divides both `F` and `G`, and because the Bézout identity expresses `x²+1` as their polynomial combination, it is an exact greatest-common-divisor certificate.

The distinct residual factors are coprime:

```text
Resultant(x(66x - 157), 11x² - 6x - 11, x) = -1771561 ≠ 0.
```

Thus no additional common root exists, even over the complex algebraic closure.

### 190.3 Shared reciprocal return and guard checks

For the shared roots, both branch return laws agree:

```text
x = I  =>  y = -I
x = -I =>  y = I
```

On Module 187, `y=x³`; on Module 178, `y=(6x+11)/(11x-6)` with nonzero guard. Both return laws were newly reexecuted at both shared roots and recover the same pairs, so the shared branch has exact bilateral scalar closure.

At `x=6/11` the Module 187 polynomial obeys

```text
G(6/11) = -157/11 ≠ 0.
```

The singular inverse-parameterization boundary is not an admitted common root. The local guarded division remains at stable HNAN0 with its no-write semantics, while exact contradiction evidence may cancel only the active inconsistent branch (Modules 188–189).

### 190.4 Branch-preserving consensus packet

```text
Consensus190 == List(
  Branch178 == F(x) == 0 with its coupled-tensor gate and full lineage,
  Branch187 == G(x) == 0 with its cubic-return gate and full lineage,
  SharedPolynomial == x² + 1,
  SharedPairs == {(I,-I),(-I,I)},
  BezoutResidual == 0,
  DistinctResultant == -1771561 != 0,
  BranchSpecificRoots == retained in their original branches,
  CompleteOriginalTrees == retained, not identified by a scalar gcd alone,
  NativeMatrixScalarBroadcast == HNAN0 until typed admission,
  CanonicalHash72Hash216Mutation == none
)
```

The common-root certificate is an exact **scalar branch-intersection theorem**. It does not assert full ordered native equivalence between the Module 166–177 Lo Shu/rational manifold trees and the Module 178–189 scalar gates; that cross-family constructor trace is a separate obligation.
